* All meetings, except closed sessions, are open to the public.  The Board welcomes citize ns’ comments.  The public participation 
portion of the meeting is limited to one -half hour for discussion of agenda items and one -half hour for non -agenda items.  Each 
person will be allowed to speak for up to three minutes, except where the number of spea kers exceeds the time limit.  In those 
instances, the President may reduce the time limit for each speaker in order to accommodate all of the individuals who wish t o 
address the Board of Education.  In any event, no one’s right to address the Board will be  denied.

President  Philippart  called  the  workshop  to  order  at  5:00  p.m.   In  addition  to  Ms. 
Philippart,  the  following  Board  members  were  present:   Mrs.  Edmunds,  Mrs.  Fleming, 
Mr. Hauff, Mr. Miletti, Mr. Schmidt, and Ms. Underwood.  Administrators in attendance 
were Superintendent Fowler, Ms. Birmingham, Mrs. Keeling, Dr. Machesky, and and Mr. 
Witt, and approximately 3 other members of the audience.

Ms.  Philippart  inquired  on  how  many  new  teachers  the 
District has hired to date.   Mr. Witt responded that currently 
there  are  37  new  hires  and  7  additional  new  teachers  still 
need to turn in their paperwork.  He indicated that there were 
to be filled.

CURRICULUM 
Schools of Choice, Section 105C 
the  Troy  School  District,  exercising  the  option  permitted  by 
by  Public  Act  300  of  1996,  will  accept  applications  for  a 
Schools of Choice program for the 2014‐2015 school year for 
an  unlimited  number  of  9th  grade  students  who  attend  a 
school  district  in  another  county  if  that  Intermediate  School 
District  is  contiguous  with  the  Oakland  Intermediate  School 
District  boundaries  for  the  purposes  of  attending  the 
International  Academy  East  program  located  in  the  Troy 
School District. 
Mr. Schmidt asked how many students came to IA‐East in the 
2012‐2013  school  year.   Dr.  Machesky  responded  that  148 
applications were received and 52 of those students qualified.  
He  added  that  for  the  2013‐2014  school  year,  159 
applications  were  received  and  76  students  qualified  but 
stated that that number will most likely go down.

Ballot Language for 2013 Bond 
            WHEREAS,  the  School  District  is  contemplating 
undertaking  various  capital  improvements  to  facilities  within 
the  School  District  which  include  improvements  to  enhance
security  throughout  the  School  District,  other  improvements 
and  additions  to  school  buildings,  the  acquisition  and 
installation  of  technology  equipment  and  technology 
infrastructure  and  the  purchase  of  school  buses,  (collectively 
the “Projects”);  
  WHEREAS,  the  School  District  is  of  the  opinion  that  in 
submitted to the electors at a Special School Election; 
  WHEREAS,  the  School  District  is  a  Michigan  general 
powers  school  district  operating  under  the  Revised  School 
Code, as amended, and as such the School District’s elections 
are  governed  by  the  Michigan  Election  Law,  Act  116,  Public 
Acts  of  Michigan,  1954,  as  amended  (the  “Michigan  Election 
Law”); 
  WHEREAS,  the  School  District  desires  to  submit  a 
bonding proposition to the electors at a Special School Election 
to  be  held  on  November  5,  2013,  which  election  will  be 
conducted by the School District’s Election Coordinator;  
School  District  certify  the  ballot  language  for  any  proposals  to 
be voted on at a Special School Election to the School District’s 
Election Coordinator not less than seventy (70) days prior to the 
date of the Special School Election;  
above  referenced  bonding  proposition  and  to  authorize  the 
Superintendent  or  her  designee  to  certify  the  bonding 
proposition to the School District’s Election Coordinator. 
  NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE BOARD OF

SCHOOL IMPROVEMENT BOND PROPOSITION
Shall  the  Troy  School  District,  County  of  Oakland,  State  of 
Michigan,  borrow  the  sum  of  not  to  exceed  One  Hundred 
Twenty  Five  Million  ($125,000,000)  Dollars  and  issue  its 
general  obligation  unlimited  tax  bonds  therefore,  in  one  or 
more  series,  for  the  purpose  of  paying  for  the  cost  of  the 
following projects: 
 Remodeling,  equipping,  re‐equipping, 
furnishing,  re‐furnishing  school 
buildings and other facilities to enhance 
security and for other purposes;  
 Acquiring  and  installing  technology 
equipment  and  technology 
infrastructure  in  school  buildings  and 
other facilities; 
 Constructing  additions  to  school 
buildings; and 
 Preparing,  developing  and  improving 
sites  at  school  buildings  and  other 
facilities  and  the  purchase  of  school 
buses? 
Yes:                         7 
No:                         0 
The  maximum  number  of  years  the  bonds  may  be 
outstanding,  exclusive  of  refunding,  is  not  more  than  fifteen 
(15) years; the estimated millage that will be levied to pay the 
$1.84 per $1,000 of taxable value); and the estimated simple 
average  annual  millage  that  will  be  required  to  retire  the 
bonds over fifteen (15) years is 2.93 mills annually ($2.93 per 
$1,000 of taxable value). 
If  approved  by  the  voters,  the  bonds  will  be  guaranteed  by 
the  State  under  the  School  Bond  Qualification  and  Loan 
Program  (the  “Program”).   The  School  District  currently  has 
$93,605,000  of  qualified  bonds  outstanding  and  $0  of 
qualified  loans  outstanding  under  the  Program.   The  School 
District  does  not  expect  to  borrow  from  the  Program  to  pay 
debt  service  on  these  bonds.   The  estimated  computed 
  5 August 6, 2013 
15
proposed bonds in the first
 year is 1.84 mills (which is equal to 
millage rate required to be levied to pay the proposed bonds 
circumstances 
(Pursuant to State law, expenditure of bond proceeds must be 
audited,  and  the  proceeds  cannot  be  used  for  teacher, 
administrator  or  employee  salaries,  repair  or  maintenance 
costs or other operating expenses.) 
Board  members  discussed  Detroit’s  recent  bankruptcy  filing 
and  wondered  what  impact  it  might  have  on  surrounding 
school districts and the selling of bonds.

TROY, MICHIGAN 
   MINUTES   AUGUST 21, 2013 
SPECIAL BOARD MEETING       ADMINISTRATION BUILDING  
  President Philippart called the meeting to order at 5:00 
p.m. 
In  addition  to  Ms.  Philippart,  present  were  Board 
members  Edmunds,  Fleming,  Hauff,  and  Schmidt.   Mr. 
Miletti  and  Ms.  Underwood  were  absent  but 
participated  via  remote  communications  during  the 
closed  session  portion  of  the  meeting.   Also  present 
were  Superintendent  Fowler;  administrators  Keeling, 
Machesky, Rajter, and Witt.  There were no members in 
the audience. 
CURRICULUM 
Resolution  Urging  Continued 
Implementation  and  Funding  of 
            WHEREAS,  education  “standards”  define 
expectations  of  what  students  should  know  at  the 
conclusion of a course of study. Standards do not dictate 
curriculum  or  prescribe  a  particular  method  of 
instruction; 
            WHEREAS,  the  Troy  Board  of  Education 
recognizes  that  in  order  for  students  to  compete  in  a 
knowledge‐based,  global  economy,  Michigan  needs 
consistent  standards  that  will  provide  appropriate 
benchmarks  for  all  students,  regardless  of  where  they 
live; 
           WHEREAS,  the  Common  Core  State  Standards 
were  developed  by  states,  for  states,  to  provide  a 
consistent set of clear K‐12 expectations that outline the 
knowledge and skills students need in English/language 
arts  and  mathematics  to  lead  to  career‐  and  college‐
readiness; 
           WHEREAS,  the  Common  Core  State  Standards 
promote  equity  by  ensuring  all  students,  no  matter 
18
PUBLIC COMMUNICATION 
Common Core State Standards 
13‐080 
None. 
It  was  moved  by  Mrs.  Edmunds  and  supported  by  Mr. 
Hauff that  
where  they  live,  are  well  prepared  with  the  skills  and 
knowledge necessary to compete with their peers in the 
United States and abroad; 
            WHEREAS,  the  Common  Core  State  Standards 
emphasize real world skills in math, reading and writing 
along  with  critical  thinking  and  problem  solving  skills 
that  better  prepare  students  for  college,  for  work  and 
for being more informed citizens; 
            WHEREAS,  the  Common  Core  State  Standards 
maintain  local  control  of  curriculum  and  teaching 
methods  and  build  upon  strengths  and  lessons  of 
current state standards; 
            WHEREAS,  local  school  districts  will  continue  to 
control  the  textbooks,  educational  materials  and 
instructional  methods  used  by  teachers  to  teach  at  or 
above  the  level  of  rigor  required  by  the  Common  Core 
State Standards; 
           WHEREAS,  the  members  of  the  Michigan  State 
Board of Education voted unanimously on June 15, 2010 
to  adopt  the  Common  Core  State  Standards  as  the 
Michigan  Core  Standards  for  mathematics  and 
English/language arts; 
           WHEREAS,  the  Michigan  Legislature  inserted 
language  into  the  Michigan  Department  of  Education 
budget  that  effectively  prohibits  Michigan  from 
participating  in  the  career‐  and  college‐ready  Common 
Core State Standards; 
           WHEREAS, the failure to implement the Common 
Core  State  Standards  will  jeopardize  the  waiver 
Michigan  received  under  the  No  Child  Left  Behind  Act, 
causing  a  majority  school  districts  in  the  state  to  fall 
below the adequate yearly progress standard; 
           WHEREAS,  Michigan  school  districts  have  already 
begun to implement the Common Core State Standards 
and  abandoning  the  implementation  of  the  standards 
will  lead  to  confusion  for  teachers  and  students  as  to 
what is expected of them; and 
            NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Troy 
Board of Education endorses the principles and goals of 
the  Common  Core  State  Standards  as  adopted  and 
strongly  urges  the  Michigan  Legislature  to  support  and 
fund  their  continued  implementation  in  Michigan’s 
public education system in order to maintain Michigan’s 
commitment to rigorous expectations for all students.

 7. A.   09.03.13 
7. CURRICULUM  
A. REPORT:  Student Learning Showcase  
Over the past three years , during the month of May , Troy 
schools participate in an exclusive event, called the Student 
Learning Showcase.  This event provides an opportunity for 
each school to showcase some of the great things they do to 
support student learning and to celebrate their successes.  Anne 
Mull, Director of Assessment and Instruction, will present a 
video summarizing this past year’s Student Learning Showcase, 
which was held on May 16th.     
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report will be presented by Ms. 
Anne Mull.  No action is required.

 10. A.   09.03.13 
10. OTHER 
A. REPORT:  Bond Update  
The informational Bond Campaign is progressing as planned. 
The boards have been delivered and are on display at every 
school as well as Central Office and the Services Building. Flyers 
have been distributed to every school and bond information is 
now on our website. A presentation schedule has begun at our 
schools and in the community, and an information podcast has 
been produced. This podcast will be shown at every curriculum 
and welcome event at every school. We would like to show it to 
you now. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Roll video.

  CONSIDERATION:  Negotiations  
The Board must pass a resolution to go into closed session  to discuss 
negotiations. Following the passage of the resolution, the Board of  
Education will go into closed session.  No additional business will take place 
after this time, other than to officially adjourn the meeting. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be it therefore res olved that, as permitted in the Michigan Open Meetings Act, Public 
Act 267, Section 8 (c), the Troy Board of Education shall go into closed session to discuss 
negotiations.
